<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **r-abhyankar** [claim](#stmt-a-abhyankar) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-abhyankar): Monomial chart from independent values and residues; completed local ring expansion -> Quasi-monomial structure
- **r-ample-model** [claim](#stmt-lem-ample-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ample-model): Relative base-point-free theorem and crepant ample model -> Sequence with relatively ample anti-canonical divisor
- **r-canonical-cone** [claim](#stmt-a-canonical-cone) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-canonical-cone): Finite generation, normality by prime inequalities, quasi-etale cyclic cover -> Klt cone with canonical generator
- **r-cone-gauss** [claim](#stmt-a-cone-gauss) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-cone-gauss): Gauss extension and equivariant log resolution -> Value and discrepancy formulas
- **r-index-hypersurface** [claim](#stmt-a-index-hypersurface) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-index-hypersurface): Plt blowup and relative monotone lc complement -> Hypersurface with uniform coefficient
- **r-initial-lc-place** [claim](#stmt-a-initial-lc-place) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-initial-lc-place): Contradiction with the hypersurface lemma on an ideal supported at the vertex -> Centred lc place detecting the base ideal
- **r-minimizer-comparison** [claim](#stmt-a-minimizer-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-minimizer-comparison): Expected vanishing criterion and multiplication embedding -> Uniform value comparison
- **r-exact-penalization** [claim](#stmt-a-exact-penalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-exact-penalization): Perturbed klt pair, monomial retraction, polynomial isolation by differential operators -> Constrained minimizer equals ordinary minimizer
- **r-leading-form** [claim](#stmt-a-leading-form) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-leading-form): Logarithmic wedge determinant in a monomial chart -> Leading top form
- **r-adapted-presentation** [claim](#stmt-a-adapted-presentation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-adapted-presentation): Subduction using log Izumi -> Initial-ideal presentation
- **r-canonical-specialization** [claim](#stmt-a-canonical-specialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-canonical-specialization): Weight family with flat special fibre -> Canonical generator on the graded spectrum
- **r-boundary-specialization** [claim](#stmt-a-boundary-specialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-boundary-specialization): Homogenized component ideals in one weight approximation -> Specialized boundary
- **r-flattening** [claim](#stmt-a-flattening) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-flattening): Lexicographic valuation and small weight perturbation -> Single Abhyankar valuation
- **r-gauss-lifts** [claim](#stmt-a-gauss-lifts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-gauss-lifts): Rational polyhedral weight system and lifting of primes -> Quotient pair klt log Fano
- **r-weak-essentiality** [claim](#stmt-a-weak-essentiality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-weak-essentiality): Contradiction with maximal torus rank via a new minimizer and flattening -> Weak essentiality bound
- **r-push-threshold** [claim](#stmt-b-push-threshold) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-push-threshold): Snc computation on a resolution -> Attained push minimum
- **r-ordinary-approximation** [claim](#stmt-b-ordinary-approximation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-ordinary-approximation): Ample reserve and general member -> Ordinary klt representative
- **r-controlled-extraction** [claim](#stmt-b-controlled-extraction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-controlled-extraction): Adjoint MMP extracting prescribed divisors -> Extraction model
- **r-primary-minimization** [claim](#stmt-b-primary-minimization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-primary-minimization): Unique-prime model and generalized threshold ACC -> Uniform interval
- **r-principal-denominators** [claim](#stmt-b-principal-denominators) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-principal-denominators): Bounded monotone lc complement -> Bounded denominators
- **r-push-presentation** [claim](#stmt-b-push-presentation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-push-presentation): Generic member over enlarged constants, canonical bundle formula, endpoint by affine interpolation -> Nef moduli downstairs
- **r-scale-bad-primes** [claim](#stmt-b-scale-bad-primes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-scale-bad-primes): Monomial weights bound and extraction -> Extraction of bad primes
- **r-scale-birationality** [claim](#stmt-b-scale-birationality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-scale-birationality): Adjoint MMP and polarized birationality theorem -> Birational multiple
- **r-scale-collapse** [claim](#stmt-b-scale-collapse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-scale-collapse): Threshold, adjoint MMP, degree comparison on generic fibre -> Contraction to a lower base
- **r-scale-inheritance** [claim](#stmt-b-scale-inheritance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-scale-inheritance): Valuative push and principal moduli on the initial field -> Next-level invariants
- **r-scale-terminal** [claim](#stmt-b-scale-terminal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-scale-terminal): Constant choice and boundedness theorem -> Terminal estimates
- **r-horizontal-estimates** [claim](#stmt-b-horizontal-estimates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-horizontal-estimates): Cone theorem and polarized threshold -> Order bounds by degree
- **r-pure-upper** [claim](#stmt-b-pure-upper) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-pure-upper): Induction down the tower -> Pure upper estimate
- **r-class-lattices** [claim](#stmt-b-class-lattices) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-class-lattices): Simultaneous resolution, stratification and Hodge theory -> Lattice types
- **r-principal-descent** [claim](#stmt-b-principal-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-principal-descent): Hilbert 90 -> Arithmetic witness
- **r-twist-saturation** [claim](#stmt-b-twist-saturation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-twist-saturation): Class obstruction removal and vanishing theorem sections -> Opposite twist sections
- **r-coefficient-dimension** [claim](#stmt-b-coefficient-dimension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-coefficient-dimension): Weighted Taylor truncation -> Dimension bounds
- **r-actual-probes** [claim](#stmt-b-actual-probes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-actual-probes): Birational system and twist saturation -> Independent probes
- **r-pure-descent** [claim](#stmt-b-pure-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-pure-descent): Integral pole orders on fibres -> Descent to the terminal field
- **r-guard-model** [claim](#stmt-b-guard-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-guard-model): Guard boundary and extraction -> Bounded marked models
- **r-bounded-guard-boundary** [claim](#stmt-b-bounded-guard-boundary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-bounded-guard-boundary): MMP to a nef model and base-point-free theorem -> Bounded auxiliary boundary
- **r-generic-marked-fano** [claim](#stmt-b-generic-marked-fano) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-generic-marked-fano): Spreading and constructibility -> Generic fibre Fano type
- **r-marked-cone-test** [claim](#stmt-b-marked-cone-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-marked-cone-test): Sweeping curves on facets of the effective cone -> Finite cone test
- **r-slow-charge-bound** [claim](#stmt-b-slow-charge-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-slow-charge-bound): Closed projected cone and degree-zero eigenfunction -> Charge bound
- **r-bounded-axes-estimates** [claim](#stmt-b-bounded-axes-estimates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-slow-charge-bound-2): Width lower bound by contradiction and cutoff counting -> Bounded axes estimates
- **r-finite-barrier** [claim](#stmt-c-finite-barrier) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-finite-barrier): Finite generation and exact subduction -> Finite barrier test
- **r-ordered-monomialization** [claim](#stmt-c-ordered-monomialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-ordered-monomialization): Resolution and least-weight monomial -> Monomial valuation
- **r-log-determinant** [claim](#stmt-c-log-determinant) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-log-determinant): Logarithmic differential matrix reduction -> Nonnegativity and equality case
- **r-invariantization** [claim](#stmt-c-invariantization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-invariantization): Gauss valuation comparison -> Invariant valuation
- **r-secondary-flattening** [claim](#stmt-c-flattening) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-flattening): Lexicographic valuation and monomialization -> Real monomial ray
- **r-skeleton** [claim](#stmt-c-skeleton) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-skeleton): Retraction and primary ideal argument -> Skeleton reduction
- **r-optimization** [claim](#stmt-c-optimization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-optimization): Compactness of sublevels and polyhedral continuation -> Attained optimizer
- **r-relative-budget** [claim](#stmt-c-relative-budget) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-relative-budget): Variation along flattened path and tie rule -> Relative inequality
- **r-strict-budget** [claim](#stmt-c-strict-budget) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-strict-budget): Finite subcover on the normalized skeleton -> Strict rational budget
- **r-trace-existence** [claim](#stmt-c-trace-existence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-trace-budget-2): Multiplier ideal to test ideal comparison -> Nonzero Cartier trace
- **r-trace-initial-survival** [claim](#stmt-c-trace-initial-survival) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-trace-initial-survival): Frobenius basis expansion -> Value inequality and equality criterion
- **r-trace-degree** [claim](#stmt-c-trace-degree) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-trace-degree): Trace on the canonical generator and normality -> Degree bound
- **r-trace-taylor-flags** [claim](#stmt-c-trace-taylor-flags) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-trace-taylor-flags): Taylor initial computation -> Full initial-field survival
- **r-trace-finite-data** [claim](#stmt-c-trace-finite-data) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-trace-finite-data): Spreading out finite data -> Retained data
- **r-trace-budget** [claim](#stmt-c-trace-budget) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-trace-budget): Strict budget as trace hypothesis and accounting of factors -> Budgeted outputs
- **r-cheap-box-count** [claim](#stmt-c-cheap-box-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-cheap-box-count): Coset counting in lattice boxes -> Rational expression
- **r-cheap-collapse** [claim](#stmt-c-cheap-collapse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-cheap-collapse): Slope bound along segment to zero -> Collapsed valuation
- **r-cheap-exact-zero** [claim](#stmt-c-cheap-exact-zero) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-cheap-exact-zero): Ultraproduct algebra -> Exact zero value
- **r-cheap-two-sided** [claim](#stmt-c-cheap-two-sided) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-cheap-two-sided): Mandatory trace factors and lower trace inequality -> Two-sided bound
- **r-layer-dimension** [claim](#stmt-c-layer-dimension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-layer-dimension): Upper count and probe box lower bound -> Layer dimension
- **r-rank-increase** [claim](#stmt-c-rank-increase) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-rank-increase): Taylor order polytope and shifted traces -> New independent initials
- **r-layer-slopes** [claim](#stmt-c-layer-slopes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-layer-slopes): Slope mechanism with rectangular boxes -> Slope bound
- **r-integral-rounding** [claim](#stmt-c-integral-rounding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-integral-rounding): Dirichlet approximation and slope integration -> Integral parameters
- **r-divisorial-index** [claim](#stmt-c-divisorial-index) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-divisorial-index): Finite value group image by box counting -> Divisorial multiple
- **r-threshold-proposition** [claim](#stmt-prop-birational-threshold) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-divisorial-index-2): Contradiction: divisor of small discrepancy against epsilon-lc -> Uniform threshold bound
- **r-threshold-to-complement** [claim](#stmt-lem-threshold-to-complement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-threshold-to-complement): Bertini on a log resolution and Chen's equivalence -> Complex complements
- **r-field-descent** [claim](#stmt-lem-field-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-field-descent): Descent to a countable subfield and log resolution inequalities -> Result over any field
- **r-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-threshold-to-complement): Combine reduction and descent -> Main theorem
- **r-finite-coefficients** [claim](#stmt-cor-finite-coefficients) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ample-model-2): Crepant ample model and Chen's equivalence -> Finite-coefficient complements

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-bounded-klt-index**: For fixed dimension and fixed positive rational lower discrepancy bound, one index works for every Fano contraction in the stated class: at each closed base point there is an effective boundary and a neighbourhood over which the pair is klt and the index multiple of the adjoint divisor is linearly trivial.
- **inv-lct-lower-bound**: For birational contractions of Fano type with nef anti-canonical divisor and bounded lower discrepancy, the log canonical threshold of the pulled-back maximal ideal of a closed base point is bounded below by a positive constant depending only on dimension and the discrepancy bound.
- **inv-finite-coefficient-index**: For pairs with coefficients in a fixed finite rational set, of Fano type with nef anti-log-canonical divisor over the complex numbers, there is a monotone klt complement of index bounded in terms of dimension, discrepancy bound and the coefficient set.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-positive-characteristic**: Apply the bounded index to varieties over a field of positive or mixed characteristic. -> The theorem is stated only over algebraically closed fields of characteristic zero; the positive-characteristic reductions in the proof are auxiliary calculations, not a result in that setting.
- **anti-lc-instead-of-klt**: Treat any lc complement of bounded index as a klt complement by lowering boundary coefficients. -> Lowering coefficients of an lc complement need not keep the adjoint divisor linearly trivial, so the bounded klt statement does not follow from lc complement theory alone.
- **anti-computable-index**: Read the index as an explicitly computable function of dimension and discrepancy bound. -> The result asserts only existence of the integer; the proof argues by contradiction on a sequence, so no explicit value or procedure is produced.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main results are existence statements for a bounded index, proved by assuming a sequence of counterexamples with threshold tending to zero and deriving a contradiction through valuations, graded rings and positive-characteristic trace arguments. No finite procedure that computes the index, the boundary or the neighbourhood is given, so there is no finite constructive object to execute and test.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves an existence theorem for bounded klt complements. It gives no procedure, so there is no software component to build or specify. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper concerns simulation, game state, or interactive systems. It is purely birational geometry. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No tool, checker, or utility can be derived from the result. The proof is a contradiction argument built on valuations and Cartier trace, not a computation that could be packaged. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main results are non-constructive. They give a bounded index N(d, epsilon) obtained from a contradiction sequence, with no explicit steps and no complexity statement. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper introduces no data representation, container, or index structure. Its objects are varieties, divisors, and valuations. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no numerical kernel, arithmetic workload, or parallel structure in the paper that could be mapped to hardware. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No hardness assumption, key structure, or security property appears. The field is algebraically closed of characteristic zero, which carries no cryptographic content. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no learning problem, loss, or data distribution. Minimizing normalized volume over valuations is a theoretical device, not a trainable objective. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Normalized-volume minimization over valuations is used only inside the proof to obtain a minimizer. It is not a finite or computable optimization problem and gives no solver or model. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no notion of nodes, messages, consensus, or concurrency. |
| other | True | weak | inspirational | inferred | The result is a hypothesis-sensitive theorem that downstream mathematical work can cite for uniform index bounds. It is useful as a citable statement within algebraic geometry, not as an engineered artifact. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-characteristic-zero** (hypothesis): The bounded index is proved only over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero; positive-characteristic reductions in the proof are auxiliary calculations on retained finite data, not a result in that setting. Blocked use: ['Applying the bounded klt complement index to varieties over fields of positive or mixed characteristic.', 'Applying it over a field that is not algebraically closed without first extending constants.']
- **limit-epsilon-lc-hypothesis** (hypothesis): The uniform index requires a fixed positive lower bound on log discrepancies of the total space and a fixed dimension; the index depends only on these two data. Blocked use: ['Citing the bound for families whose discrepancy lower bound degenerates to zero.', 'Citing the bound with varying dimension and a single index.']
- **limit-klt-versus-lc** (formal_scope): Bounded lc complements are not bounded klt complements: an lc complement may have places of zero discrepancy and lowering its coefficients need not keep the adjoint divisor linearly trivial. Blocked use: ['Treating any bounded-index lc complement as a klt complement by reducing boundary coefficients.', 'Reading the klt conclusion as an epsilon-lc complement statement.']
- **limit-nonconstructive-index** (nonconstructive): The threshold proposition is proved by contradiction along an assumed violating sequence, so only existence of the index is obtained; no explicit value, boundary or neighbourhood is produced by a finite procedure. Blocked use: ['Using a numerical value of the index in code, resource sizing or loop bounds.', 'Presenting the proof as an algorithm that outputs a complement.']
- **limit-finite-rational-coefficients** (hypothesis): The pair version is stated over the complex numbers, for quasi-projective contractions of Fano type with nef anti-log-canonical divisor and boundary coefficients in a fixed finite rational set; the index depends on that set. Blocked use: ['Applying the pair version to real or unbounded coefficient sets with the same index.', 'Applying it to non-quasi-projective contractions or to varieties not of Fano type over the base.']
- **limit-index-versus-auxiliary-degree** (edge_case): The auxiliary lc degree fixed in the cone construction is distinct from the final klt complement index; it is not asserted to be a klt complement index. Blocked use: ['Reading the auxiliary degree used in the proof as the bound on the klt complement index.']
- **limit-local-dependence-of-boundary** (edge_case): Only the index is uniform: the boundary and the neighbourhood of the base point may depend on the contraction and the point, and shrinking the base is allowed. Blocked use: ['Assuming a single boundary or a single neighbourhood works for a whole family or for all base points at once.']
- **limit-field-descent-multiple** (approximation): Passing from the complex numbers to an arbitrary algebraically closed field of characteristic zero is obtained only with a fixed multiple of the complex index, not the same index. Blocked use: ['Claiming the identical integer works over every algebraically closed field of characteristic zero.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For fixed dimension <i>d</i> and positive rational <i>ϵ</i>, we prove that every <i>ϵ</i>-log-canonical Fano contraction over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero admits, near each closed base point, a klt complement of index bounded only by <i>d</i> and <i>ϵ</i>. Over ℂ, we also obtain monotone klt complements for Fano type pairs with nef anti-log-canonical divisor and coefficients in a fixed finite rational set. This proves the finite-rational-coefficient form of Shokurov's bounded-klt-complement conjecture.

| [stmt-c-integral-rounding](#stmt-c-integral-rounding) | proposition | `c:integral-rounding` | 70 | For every absolute tolerance $\sigma>0$, a rank increase followed by a bounded integral scaling and rounding of the new parameters changes a small objective bound according to |
| [stmt-c-divisorial-index](#stmt-c-divisorial-index) | proposition | `c:divisorial-index` | 71 | At a final optimizer with $d$ independent probe initials and integral probe parameters, there is a uniformly bounded positive integer $e$ such that $ew$ is a positive integral multiple of a prime-divi |

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Let $f\colon X\to Z$ be a projective contraction of normal varieties, meaning that $f$ is surjective and $f_*\mathcal O_X=\mathcal O_Z$. A complement near a closed point $z\in Z$ is an effective rational boundary $B$ for which the adjoint divisor $K_X+B$ becomes torsion on the inverse image of a neighbourhood of $z$. More precisely, an index $N$ records that $N(K_X+B)$ is integral Cartier and linearly trivial there. The complement is klt if every log discrepancy of $(X,B)$ is positive. We ask whether one index works for all contractions of a fixed dimension whose singularities have a fixed positive lower discrepancy bound.

When $K_X$ is rational Cartier and $-K_X$ is ample over $Z$, we call $f$ a *Fano contraction*. Complements turn this positivity into an adjoint divisor with controlled torsion. The distinction between lc and klt complements is essential: an lc complement permits zero discrepancies, whereas a klt complement must keep every discrepancy strictly positive. We prove that a uniform positive bound for the singularities of $X$ suffices to achieve the latter conclusion with a bounded index.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *For every positive integer $d$ and positive rational number $\epsilon$ there is a positive integer $N=N(d,\epsilon)$ with the following property. Let $k$ be an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, and let $f\colon X\to Z$ be a projective surjective morphism of normal integral $k$-varieties such that $f_*\mathcal O_X=\mathcal O_Z$, $\dim X=d$, $K_X$ is $\mathbb Q$-Cartier, $X$ is $\epsilon$-lc, and $-K_X$ is ample over $Z$. For every closed point $z\in Z$ there are an effective $\mathbb Q$-divisor $B$ on $X$ and a Zariski open neighbourhood $U$ of $z$ such that $(X,B)$ is klt over $U$ and $$N(K_X+B)|_{f^{-1}(U)}\sim 0$$ is an integral Cartier divisor.*

</div>

The morphism in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> need not have relative Picard number one, nor need its general fibre have positive dimension. The displayed linear equivalence means triviality of the associated line bundle on $f^{-1}(U)$, after the indicated shrinking; it is stronger than numerical triviality. Both $B$ and $U$ may depend on $f$ and $z$, whereas $N$ does not.

A variety is of Fano type over its base if it admits an effective $\mathbb Q$-boundary $\Delta$ for which $(X,\Delta)$ is klt and $-(K_X+\Delta)$ is relatively ample. Equivalent versions using a nef and big anti-log-canonical divisor may be converted to this one by a small boundary perturbation.

The proof uses the equivalence between bounded klt complements and a local threshold statement established by Chen (Chen 2025, Theorem 1.6) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Chen](#ref-Chen)]. The birational, boundary-free case of that threshold statement remains the main issue. We formulate it in Proposition <a href="#prop:birational-threshold" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:birational-threshold">3</a> and prove it by contradiction. The role of bounded lc complements is different: they supply an auxiliary linear system of a fixed degree $n$. Throughout the proof, this $n$ is distinguished from the final klt complement index $N$.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Earlier results and the finite-coefficient conjecture

Shokurov introduced complements in his work on threefold log flips (Shokurov 1993, sec. 5) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[ShokurovFlips](#ref-ShokurovFlips)] and formulated their boundedness systematically in his surface theory (Shokurov 2000, Conjecture 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[ShokurovSurfaces](#ref-ShokurovSurfaces)]. Prokhorov–Shokurov developed the passage from global complements to local ones and the higher-dimensional inductive program (Prokhorov and Shokurov 2001, 2009) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[ProkhorovShokurovFirst](#ref-ProkhorovShokurovFirst), [ProkhorovShokurov](#ref-ProkhorovShokurov)]. Shokurov’s later treatment includes a bounded lc complement theorem assuming the existence of a klt real complement (Shokurov 2020, Theorem 3) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[ShokurovComplements](#ref-ShokurovComplements)]. Here the klt condition concerns the input real complement; the bounded-index output is lc. An lc complement may have places of zero discrepancy. Reducing its boundary coefficients need not preserve the linear triviality of the adjoint divisor, so keeping the complement klt requires an additional argument.

Birkar proved boundedness of lc complements for Fano type fibrations with hyperstandard coefficients (Birkar 2019, Theorems 1.7 and 1.8) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[BirkarComplements](#ref-BirkarComplements)], and boundedness of klt complements for boundary-free Fano fibrations over curves (Birkar 2025, Corollary 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[BirkarFibrations](#ref-BirkarFibrations)]. Chen related bounded klt complements to uniform local hypersurface thresholds and established the conjecture for bases of dimension at most two (Chen 2025, Theorem 1.6 and Corollary 1.8) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Chen](#ref-Chen)].

Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> also gives the following consequence in Chen’s complex quasi-projective setting.

<a id="stmt-cor-finite-coefficients"></a>
<div id="cor:finite-coefficients" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2**. *Fix a positive integer $d$, a real number $\epsilon>0$, and a finite set $I\subset[0,1]\cap\mathbb Q$. There is a positive integer $M=M(d,\epsilon,I)$ with the following property. Let $(X,\Delta)$ be an $\epsilon$-lc pair of dimension $d$ with coefficients in $I$, over $\mathbb C$. Let $f\colon X\to Z$ be a contraction of quasi-projective normal varieties, assume that $X$ is of Fano type over $Z$, and assume that $-(K_X+\Delta)$ is nef over $Z$. For every closed $z\in Z$, there is an effective $\mathbb Q$-divisor $\Delta^+\ge\Delta$ such that, near $f^{-1}(z)$, the pair $(X,\Delta^+)$ is klt and $M(K_X+\Delta^+)$ is Cartier and linearly trivial after shrinking the base about $z$.*

</div>

The proof, using Chen’s equivalence and a crepant ample model, is given in Section <a href="#sec:reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reduction">2</a>.

Corollary <a href="#cor:finite-coefficients" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:finite-coefficients">2</a> is the finite-rational-coefficient form of Shokurov’s bounded-klt-complement conjecture stated in (Chen 2025, Conjecture 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Chen](#ref-Chen)]. The same equivalence yields the local hypersurface assertions in (Chen 2025, Conjectures 1.3 and 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Chen](#ref-Chen)]. The conclusion concerns klt complements; the stronger requirement of an $\epsilon$-lc complement in (Chen 2025, Remark 1.2(1)) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Chen](#ref-Chen)] is not asserted here.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Methods and related work

Our first tools come from normalized-volume minimization. Li established properness estimates (Li 2018) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[LiProperness](#ref-LiProperness)]; Blum proved existence of a minimizer (Blum 2018) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[BlumExistence](#ref-BlumExistence)]; Xu proved its quasi-monomiality (Xu 2020) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[XuQuasimonomial](#ref-XuQuasimonomial)]; and Xu–Zhuang proved uniqueness and finite generation (Xu and Zhuang 2021, 2025) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[XuZhuangUniqueness](#ref-XuZhuangUniqueness), [XuZhuangStable](#ref-XuZhuangStable)]. Li–Xu identifies the minimizing Reeb valuation on the resulting K-semistable cone (Li and Xu 2018) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[LiXuStable](#ref-LiXuStable)]. We use this ordinary klt theory after proving an additional exactness statement: one fixed small rational perturbation has its actual global minimizer on the prescribed lc constraint. This is Proposition <a href="#a:exact-penalization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="a:exact-penalization">13</a>; its constants are allowed to depend on the individual germ and ideals.

The projective stage combines minimal models and Mori dream spaces (Birkar et al. 2010; Hu and Keel 2000) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[BCHM](#ref-BCHM), [HuKeel](#ref-HuKeel)], Ambro’s canonical bundle formula (Ambro 2004, 2005) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[AmbroBoundary](#ref-AmbroBoundary), [AmbroModuli](#ref-AmbroModuli)], and Birkar’s boundedness and threshold theorems (Birkar 2021, 2023, 2025) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[BirkarBAB](#ref-BirkarBAB), [BirkarPolarised](#ref-BirkarPolarised), [BirkarFibrations](#ref-BirkarFibrations)]. The needed uniformity concerns both divisor classes and effective representatives. We retain those representatives in marked bounded families before passing to generic fibres or taking limits.

The final stage uses Takagi’s comparison between multiplier and test ideals (Takagi 2004) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Takagi](#ref-Takagi)], in the dense-open formulation recalled by de Fernex–Docampo–Takagi–Tucker (Fernex et al. 2015, Theorem 11) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[DFDTT](#ref-DFDTT)], together with Schwede’s Cartier-algebra formulation (Schwede 2011) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[SchwedeNonQG](#ref-SchwedeNonQG)]. These results supply trace generation for a fixed rational pair. The proof below additionally retains the finite output spaces and their valuation bounds through two successive liftings. Related local Cartier-threshold bounds under a positive normalized-volume lower bound appear in (Han, Liu, and Qi 2024, Theorem 1.7) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[HanLiuQiLocalVolumes](#ref-HanLiuQiLocalVolumes)].

The companion article (OpenAI 2026, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[OpenAICartier](#ref-OpenAICartier)] proves a uniform Cartier-section threshold for rational-boundary Fano type contractions by an independent character and pinning argument. Its endpoint is a Cartier divisor through a prescribed base point with a uniformly lc pullback. The proof here develops the complement index directly through the birational threshold and the trace estimates above.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Outline of the proof

The reduction in Section <a href="#sec:reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reduction">2</a> asks for a uniform lower bound on the log canonical threshold of the pullback of a closed base point’s maximal ideal in the birational, zero-boundary case. A sequence violating this bound has divisors whose orders on that ideal are arbitrarily large compared with their discrepancies. We will construct a prime divisor of discrepancy less than $\epsilon$. Small discrepancy at a real valuation is not by itself enough, since rescaling the valuation also rescales its discrepancy. The proof must therefore control both discrepancy and the eventual divisor normalization.

*From concentration to a graded model.* After shrinking each base to an affine neighbourhood of $z$ and replacing the sequence by its crepant ample models, Sections <a href="#a:cone" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="a:cone">3</a>–<a href="#a:essential" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="a:essential">6</a> encode anticanonical sections in an affine cone. Its bounded-degree lc system has a centred lc place detecting the concentration of the base ideal. We minimize normalized volume subject to this lc equality. Proposition <a href="#a:exact-penalization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="a:exact-penalization">13</a> shows that the constrained minimizer is exactly the ordinary minimizer of a small effective klt perturbation. This permits ordinary stable degeneration to produce a finitely generated graded ring, while a comparison at the minimizer preserves concentration of the base ideal.

Compatible further filtrations let us maximize the rank of the torus whose characters record the occurring valuation degrees. Each successive valuation remains realized on the original cone. The resulting projective quotient carries a klt pair $(S,B)$ and an effective ample rational divisor $H\sim_{\mathbb Q}-(K_S+B)$ for which $(S,B+H)$ is lc. At each lc place of this latter pair, replacing $H$ by any effective rationally linearly equivalent divisor increases its order by at most a factor $1+\eta$, where $\eta\to0$ along the sequence. This is the weak essentiality property of Proposition <a href="#a:weak-essentiality" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="a:weak-essentiality">20</a>.

We also keep actual sections on $X$. Choose a general degree-$n$ anticanonical section $h$, and let $B_h$ be one $n$-th of its effective section divisor, so $(X,B_h)$ is lc. For $m\in n\mathbb Z_{\ge0}$, dividing degree-$m$ anticanonical sections by $h^{m/n}$ gives a space of rational functions $V_m\subset k(X)$. The normalized cone valuation restricts to a valuation $v$ with $A_{X,B_h}(v)=0$. The cone valuation assigns to a section the *cost* $b(F)=m+v(F)$ of the corresponding $F\in V_m$; the assigned degree $m$ is retained in this notation. Costs are nonnegative, $V_0=k[Z]$, and $v(\mathfrak m_z)\to\infty$ along the sequence. These systems and the exact discrepancy identity below are constructed in Subsection <a href="#a:angular" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="a:angular">6.4</a>.

*Upper counts and independent sections.* Sections <a href="#b:discrepancy-push" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="b:discrepancy-push">7</a>–<a href="#b:bounded-axes" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="b:bounded-axes">10</a> use weak essentiality to control these section spaces. A finite tower of projective contractions removes one small scale of the residual field $k(S)$ at a time. Horizontal discrepancy bounds give divisor-order estimates on each relative field. Together with the torus directions, these fields determine $d$ widths $W_j$: independent rational functions in direction $j$ can be obtained at degree and cost $O(W_j)$.

The two needed estimates go in opposite directions. At any fixed physical degree, the sum of initial spaces through cost $T$ has dimension at most a uniform multiple of $\prod_j(1+T/W_j)$. Monomials in actual sections with independent initials give lower box bounds at comparable degree and cost. Here an initial is the least-valued part of a function in the associated graded field. The directions whose widths stay bounded need a further estimate: through a slowly growing cutoff, cost is bounded by a uniform multiple of physical degree, and the dimension is $O((1+m)^r)$, where $r$ is their number. To prove this, Section <a href="#b:bounded-axes" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="b:bounded-axes">10</a> retains effective divisor representatives in a marked projective family. An obstruction would produce a degree-zero section of bounded positive cost, contradicting concentration of the entire base maximal ideal.

*A small discrepancy with controlled divisor normalization.* The connection back to the singularities of $X$ is $$A_X(w)=A_{X,B_h}(w)+
       \sup_{\substack{m\in n\mathbb Z_{>0}\\0\ne F\in V_m}}
                          \frac{-w(F)}m$$ for quasi-monomial valuations centred on $X$; this is <a href="#eq:A13" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:A13">[eq:A13]</a>. Thus inequalities $w(F)+B_*m\ge q\,b(F)$, with $B_*,q>0$, bound the second term by $B_*$. In degree zero they give nonnegative values on $k[Z]$ and positive values on $\mathfrak m_z$, forcing the centre to lie over $z$. Sections <a href="#c:optimization-section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="c:optimization-section">11</a>–<a href="#c:rank-section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="c:rank-section">14</a> seek such valuations with small $A_{X,B_h}(w)$, while preserving independent initial coordinates. The fractional-form discrepancy $A_\theta$ used there is precisely $A_{X,B_h}$.

The optimization has a finite test and compact sublevels on a monomial skeleton. A suitably chosen optimizer gives a strict divisorial budget on a geometric generic fibre of the full initial field, which retains all homogeneous degrees, rather than just residue classes. In suitable positive-characteristic reductions, Cartier trace converts this budget into sections of controlled degree and value. Relative trace survival is first lifted to the full initial field and then to the original field; the finite output spaces are fixed before reduction to positive characteristic.

Comparing these trace outputs with the upper counts controls both changes of coordinate values and growth of the number of independent initials. First the bounded-width coordinates acquire value zero. A further trace argument then gives two-sided bounds for section values. Through the remaining width layers, independent initials are added and their values are rounded to integers, preserving all earlier constraints. Once the initials form a full transcendence basis, the section counts bound the denominator of the entire value group. A bounded multiple of the resulting valuation is an integral multiple of a prime divisor. The final choice of tolerances and scaling makes that divisor’s discrepancy less than $\epsilon$, completing the contradiction.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Conventions

All varieties in characteristic zero are over an algebraically closed field. After Section <a href="#sec:reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reduction">2</a> we work over $\mathbb C$ through Section <a href="#b:bounded-axes" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="b:bounded-axes">10</a>. In Section <a href="#c:optimization-section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="c:optimization-section">11</a> we descend the contradiction sequence and its specified data to a countable algebraically closed field of characteristic zero; later constructions allow the stated extensions of constants. From Section <a href="#c:trace-section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="c:trace-section">12</a>, positive-characteristic reductions are auxiliary trace calculations on retained finite data: the chosen valuations, optimization, and strict discrepancy assertions remain in characteristic zero. A contraction is a projective surjective morphism with connected fibres in the sense $f_*\mathcal O_X=\mathcal O_Z$.

We use log discrepancies. For a prime divisor $E$ on a smooth birational model $\pi\colon Y\to X$, $$A_{X,B}(E)=1+\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E\bigl(K_Y-\pi^*(K_X+B)\bigr).$$ Discrepancies, divisor orders, and ideal orders are extended homogeneously to positive real multiples of valuations. The order of an ideal is the minimum of the orders of local generators. The notation $A_X$ means $A_{X,0}$. For a nonzero ideal $\mathfrak a$ on a klt variety, $$\mathop{\mathrm{lct}}(X;\mathfrak a)=
 \inf_{E(\mathfrak a)>0}\frac{A_X(E)}{E(\mathfrak a)},$$ where the infimum is over prime divisors over $X$. Rational Cartier divisors are evaluated by pullback on a model on which the valuation has a centre.

In contradiction sequences we pass to subsequences and shrink the base about the chosen point without repeating this convention. Constants called uniform are independent of the sequence index after these operations; their allowed geometric dependencies will be stated. The number of contractions, scale layers, and rank increases is bounded. Small positive constants chosen before a limit are kept separate from quantities tending to zero along the sequence. The original dimension is $d$; the first section cone has dimension $p=d+1$. Characteristic primes in the trace arguments are denoted by $\mathfrak p$.

<a id="sec:reduction"></a>
### The birational threshold reduction

The central assertion is the following uniform estimate.

<a id="stmt-prop-birational-threshold"></a>
<div id="prop:birational-threshold" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:A1"></a>
**Proposition 3**. *For every positive integer $d$ and every $\epsilon>0$ there is $c(d,\epsilon)>0$ such that, over $\mathbb C$, the following holds. Let $f\colon X\to Z$ be a birational contraction of $d$-dimensional normal varieties, let $X$ be of Fano type over $Z$, and assume that $K_X$ is $\mathbb Q$-Cartier, $-K_X$ is nef over $Z$, and $X$ is $\epsilon$-lc. For each closed point $z\in Z$, $$\label{eq:A1}
 \mathop{\mathrm{lct}}\bigl(X;\mathfrak m_z\mathcal O_X\bigr)\ge c(d,\epsilon).$$*

</div>

The subsequent sections prove Proposition <a href="#prop:birational-threshold" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:birational-threshold">3</a>. We first explain precisely why it suffices.

<a id="stmt-lem-threshold-to-complement"></a>
<div id="lem:threshold-to-complement" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4**. *Proposition <a href="#prop:birational-threshold" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:birational-threshold">3</a> in all dimensions implies Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> over $\mathbb C$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-threshold-to-complement"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-threshold-to-complement</summary>

*Proof.* Shrink $Z$ to an affine neighbourhood of $z$. Choose generators of $\mathfrak m_z$ and take a log resolution of their pullback ideal and of $X$. The pullback system has a fixed divisor and a base-point-free moving system. By <a href="#eq:A1" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:A1">[eq:A1]</a>, multiplying the fixed divisor by $c(d,\epsilon)$ preserves log canonicity. For a general linear combination $h$ of the generators, Bertini’s theorem makes its moving divisor transverse to the resolution strata. Consequently, with $t=\min\{c(d,\epsilon),1/2\}$, the pair $(X,t f^*\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}(h))$ is lc near $f^{-1}(z)$; the same conclusion follows componentwise on the resolution. The divisor $\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}(h)$ passes through $z$. This is the birational boundary-zero instance of the local hypersurface assertion in (Chen 2025, Conjecture 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[Chen](#ref-Chen)].

Apply the implication (4)$\Rightarrow$(1) in (Chen 2025, Theorem 1.6) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[Chen](#ref-Chen)], for every base-dimension bound needed and with boundary coefficient set $\{0\}$. Its conclusion supplies a bounded klt complement on an $\epsilon$-lc contraction of Fano type whose anti-log-canonical divisor is relatively nef. A Fano contraction as in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> satisfies these hypotheses, using the zero boundary. Over our affine base its total space is quasi-projective, as required there. Relative linear triviality becomes actual linear triviality after shrinking about $z$ to trivialize the line bundle on the base.

The birational assertion in Chen’s formulation allows a canonical Weil divisor that is $\mathbb R$-Cartier. Such a divisor is already $\mathbb Q$-Cartier: on a finite open cover, expressing its Cartier condition in terms of finitely many integral divisors gives a finite system of rational linear equations. A real solution of this consistent system can be replaced by a rational solution, and a common denominator works on the finite cover. Finally a real positive lower discrepancy bound may be decreased to a positive rational one. Thus these harmless differences in formulation do not restrict the implication. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-lem-field-descent"></a>
<div id="lem:field-descent" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5**. *If Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> holds over $\mathbb C$ with a fixed index depending only on $d$ and $\epsilon$, it holds over every algebraically closed field of characteristic zero with a fixed multiple of that index.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-field-descent"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-field-descent</summary>

*Proof.* Work over an affine neighbourhood of $z$. Descend the morphism, the point, and the relevant canonical-divisor data to a countable algebraically closed subfield $k_0$ of the given field $k$. All defining equations require only finitely many coefficients, so such a $k_0$ exists and embeds in $\mathbb C$. Normality, the contraction property, and relative ampleness descend and ascend under these field extensions after enlarging the defining field when necessary. A log resolution defined over $k_0$ checks the discrepancy bound: its finitely many boundary coefficients, together with the smooth transverse strata, compute the same lc condition after either extension. We may therefore apply the complex assertion to this descended model.

Let $b>1$ be a fixed multiple of the complex complement index. Write $K_X$ using a rational top-degree form $\omega$ defined over $k_0$. The coherent module of relative sections of the reflexive sheaf $\mathcal O_X(-bK_X)$ has finitely many generators over the affine base. Represent these generators by rational functions $\phi_1,\ldots,\phi_s$ over $k_0$. Sections commute with flat constant-field extension; equivalently one may compute the reflexive powers on the smooth locus and use flat base change there. We do not require $bK_X$ to be Cartier.

<a id="eq:descent-fixed-part"></a>
Take a log resolution $\pi\colon Y\to X$ over $k_0$ on which the fractional ideal generated by the $\phi_i$ is principal, and its divisor together with $\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}_Y(\omega)$ and the exceptional locus has simple normal crossings. For every component $E$ of these divisors put $$a_E=\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E(\omega),\qquad
 q_E=\min_i E(\phi_i).$$ Over $\mathbb C$, the given klt complement is represented, after localizing on a neighbourhood of $z$, by a section $\phi$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}_X(\phi)=b(K_X+B)$. Its order at every $E$ whose proper image in the base contains $z$ is at least $q_E$: the coefficients expressing it in the chosen generators are regular on that neighbourhood. Klt of this complement therefore gives the strict inequalities $$\label{eq:descent-fixed-part}
 1+a_E>\frac{q_E}{b}$$ for all such components. These are finite inequalities for the descended resolution, so they remain true over $k$.

Remove from the base the proper images of the finitely many offending components that do not contain $z$. The fractional ideal is now its fixed divisor times a base-point-free system. A general $k$-linear combination $\phi=\sum_i\lambda_i\phi_i$ has a moving divisor which is smooth and transverse to the resolution strata, by Bertini in characteristic zero. Its coefficient in $$\frac{1}{b}\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}_Y(\phi)-\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}_Y(\omega)$$ is $1/b<1$; the fixed coefficients are strictly less than one by <a href="#eq:descent-fixed-part" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:descent-fixed-part">[eq:descent-fixed-part]</a>. This simple-normal-crossing subpair is klt. Pushing down gives an effective boundary $$B=\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}_X(\phi)-bK_X}{b},
 \qquad b(K_X+B)=\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}_X(\phi),$$ which is klt on a neighbourhood of $f^{-1}(z)$. Properness justifies the shrinking used above. Closing up this effective divisor in the original $X$ gives the divisor required by the theorem; its restriction to that neighbourhood is unchanged. Thus the fixed index $b$ works over $k$. ◻

</details>

<a id="subsec:contradiction-sequence"></a>
#### The contradiction sequence

Suppose Proposition <a href="#prop:birational-threshold" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:birational-threshold">3</a> fails for fixed $d>0$ and $\epsilon>0$. Choose a sequence of its data with threshold tending to zero. We will usually suppress the sequence index. Before constructing the cone, we make two reductions that preserve this failure.

<a id="stmt-lem-ample-model"></a>
<div id="lem:ample-model" class="lemma">

**Lemma 6**. *The sequence may be chosen with $-K_X$ ample over $Z$. This replacement preserves all discrepancies and the threshold in <a href="#eq:A1" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:A1">[eq:A1]</a>.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-ample-model"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-ample-model</summary>

*Proof.* Choose a klt log Fano boundary $\Delta$ over $Z$ and a positive integer $r$ with $-rK_X$ Cartier. The divisor $D=-rK_X$ is relatively nef, and for a positive integer $a$, $$aD-(K_X+\Delta)=-arK_X-(K_X+\Delta)$$ is relatively ample. The relative klt base-point-free theorem (Fujino 2009b, Theorem 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[FujinoBPF](#ref-FujinoBPF)] makes $-K_X$ semiample over $Z$. Let $g\colon X\to X^{\mathrm a}$ be its relative ample model. Since $X\to Z$ is birational, so is $g$. Choose canonical divisors from the same rational top form. For a Cartier divisor $H$ on $X^{\mathrm a}$ ample over $Z$ and a sufficiently divisible $m$, write $-mK_X=g^*H+\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}_X(\phi)$. Birationality identifies the function fields, so pushing forward gives $-mK_{X^{\mathrm a}}=H+\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}_{X^{\mathrm a}}(\phi)$. Pulling back this identity gives $$K_X=g^*K_{X^{\mathrm a}}.$$ The morphism is crepant; the new variety is $\epsilon$-lc and has ample anti-canonical divisor over $Z$. In particular it is of Fano type there. Every divisorial valuation has the same discrepancy on these two models, and its value on the ideal from $Z$ is the same. The valuative formula for the threshold proves the assertion. ◻

</details>

We henceforth assume that $Z$ is affine and $-K_X$ is ample over $Z$. By the valuative description of the failing threshold, there are prime divisors $P$ over $X$ such that <a id="eq:bad-prime-ratio"></a>$$\label{eq:bad-prime-ratio}
 \frac{P(\mathfrak m_z)}{A_X(P)}\longrightarrow\infty.$$ Here and below $P(\mathfrak m_z)$ denotes the order of $\mathfrak m_z\mathcal O_X$.

Birkar’s relative bounded lc complement theorem (Birkar 2019, Theorem 1.8) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[BirkarComplements](#ref-BirkarComplements)] applies to the pair $(X,0)$: the coefficient set is fixed, the total space is of Fano type over the base, and $-K_X$ is relatively nef. Our base has positive dimension $d$, so the stated relative theorem applies. After shrinking about $z$, it supplies an lc complement of an index bounded in terms of $d$. Taking a fixed multiple of the finitely bounded indices and then a further multiple if necessary, we obtain one integer $n>1$, independent of the sequence, for which <a id="eq:auxiliary-lc-system"></a>$$\label{eq:auxiliary-lc-system}
 \frac{1}{n}|-nK_X|\quad\text{is an lc linear system}.$$ This means that for every divisorial valuation $E$ with centre in the neighbourhood under consideration, $\frac1n E(|-nK_X|)\le A_X(E)$, where the system order is the minimum section order. One lc section already proves these inequalities for the whole system. The integer $n$ will remain fixed in the cone construction; it is not asserted to be a klt complement index.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### The finite-coefficient consequence

The crepant model also gives the consequence stated in the introduction, once Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> has been proved.

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-ample-model-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-ample-model</summary>

*Proof of Corollary <a href="#cor:finite-coefficients" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:finite-coefficients">2</a>.* We check item (2) of (Chen 2025, Theorem 1.6) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[Chen](#ref-Chen)] in every birational dimension. For its boundary-zero, relatively nef data, use the crepant ample model $g\colon X\to Y$ of Lemma <a href="#lem:ample-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:ample-model">6</a>. Apply Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> to $Y\to Z$, decreasing $\epsilon$ to a fixed positive rational number when needed. After shrinking about $z$, its complement boundary $B_Y$ is $\mathbb Q$-Cartier, since both $K_Y$ and $K_Y+B_Y$ are $\mathbb Q$-Cartier there. On this neighbourhood, $B_X=g^*B_Y$ is effective and $$K_X+B_X=g^*(K_Y+B_Y).$$ The pair upstairs is klt, and the same complement index makes its adjoint divisor Cartier and trivial. Take the effective closure of $B_X$ outside this neighbourhood if necessary. In particular the multiple used to construct the ample model does not change the complement index. This proves Chen’s item (2) for every base-dimension bound. The implication (2)$\Rightarrow$(1) of his theorem gives the assertion, with the stated dependence on $I$. ◻

</details>

In the rest of the paper we work with the contradiction sequence above, with its fixed auxiliary lc degree $n$, and prove Proposition <a href="#prop:birational-threshold" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:birational-threshold">3</a>.

<a id="a:cone"></a>
### The anticanonical cone and a distinguished lc place

We work with the contradiction sequence introduced after <a href="#eq:A1" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:A1">[eq:A1]</a>. Thus $X\to Z$ is birational, $Z$ is affine, $-K_X$ is relatively ample, $z\in Z$ is closed, and $d=\dim X>0$. The integer $n>1$ is fixed along the sequence and $n^{-1}|-nK_X|$ is an lc system. Put $p=d+1$. All valuations in this section are trivial on $k=\mathbb C$.

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### Valuations and forms

We shall use the full initial field of a valuation, as well as its residue field; these are different objects. We first record the monomial facts needed to distinguish them. The smooth monomial description and its finite-ideal refinements are standard in valuation theory; see (Jonsson and Mustaţă 2012, Proposition 3.7 and Lemma 3.6) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[JM](#ref-JM)]. We include the argument in the form needed for restrictions to subfields.

<a id="stmt-a-abhyankar"></a>
<div id="a:abhyankar" class="lemma">

**Lemma 7** (Monomial charts and restriction). *A real valuation of a function field of characteristic zero satisfying Abhyankar equality is quasi-monomial on a smooth proper model. The model can dominate a prescribed model and resolve finitely many additional functions and divisors. Its restriction to a subfunction field again satisfies Abhyankar equality. In particular, the restriction of a prime-divisor valuation is either trivial or a positive integral multiple of a prime-divisor valuation.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-a-abhyankar"><summary>Proof of stmt-a-abhyankar</summary>

*Proof.* Choose functions with rationally independent values spanning the value group over $\mathbb Q$, and functions of value zero whose residues form a transcendence basis of the residue field. Resolve the divisors of the first functions and the maps to $\mathbb P^1$ defined by the second, together with the prescribed data. The latter functions are regular and invertible at the centre. Consequently the dimension of the centre is at least the residue transcendence degree. At least as many transverse boundary components as the rational rank pass through it: the independent values of the first functions are integral combinations of their orders. Abhyankar equality forces equality in both bounds. The centre is therefore the generic point of their transverse intersection, and the normal parameters have rationally independent values.

In the completed local ring at that generic point, each nonzero function has a unique least-weight normal monomial with nonzero residue coefficient. The monomial ideal of strictly higher weight is finitely generated. The congruence to that monomial times a unit contracts to the local ring by faithful flatness. This proves the monomial formula.

For a restriction, add the Abhyankar inequalities for the restricted valuation and the valued field extension. Equality for the full field forces equality for the restriction. A nonzero subgroup of the value group $\mathbb Z$ of a prime divisor is $j\mathbb Z$, $j>0$. The restricted Abhyankar valuation of rational rank one is therefore $j$ times a prime-divisor valuation. ◻

</details>

For a rational top form $\eta$, its *form discrepancy* is $A_\eta(E)=1+\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E(\eta)$ at a prime divisor, extended by the log smooth formula to quasi-monomial valuations. We also write $a_\eta$. For a fractional form whose $r$-th power is a rational $r$-canonical form, use $1+r^{-1}\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E(\eta^{\otimes r})$. The top wedge of logarithmic normal differentials and regular tangential differentials has form discrepancy zero at the monomial valuation; multiplication by a rational function adds its value. Pair discrepancy is form discrepancy minus the value of the log canonical divisor expressed with that form. The trivial valuation has discrepancy zero. Precise leading-form formulas are proved in Lemma <a href="#a:leading-form" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="a:leading-form">14</a>.

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### The cone and its canonical generator

Fix a rational volume form $\omega$ on $k(X)=k(Z)$ and use $K_X=\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}_X(\omega)$. For an indeterminate $t$, set $$\mathcal A_m=
 \{g t^m:\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}_X(g)-mK_X\geq0\}\cup\{0\},\qquad
 \mathcal A=\bigoplus_{m\geq0}\mathcal A_m,\qquad
 W=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}\mathcal A .$$ Here $\mathcal A_0=k[Z]$. Denote the degree cocharacter by $D$, and the point of the vertex section above $z$ by $o$.

<a id="stmt-a-canonical-cone"></a>
<div id="a:canonical-cone" class="lemma">

**Lemma 8** (Canonical anticanonical cone). *The algebra $\mathcal A$ is finitely generated and normal, with fraction field $k(X)(t)$. The variety $W$ is klt, and $$\Omega=t\,\omega\wedge d\log t$$ has divisor zero on $W$. In particular it is a canonical generator, of $D$-weight one.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-a-canonical-cone"><summary>Proof of stmt-a-canonical-cone</summary>

*Proof.* Relative ampleness gives finite generation of a sufficiently divisible Veronese and finite generation of each shifted-degree module over it. Thus it gives finite generation in all degrees. The ring is the intersection of $k(X)[t]$ with the valuation rings imposing $\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_P(g)-mP(K_X)\geq0$ at strict prime divisors $P$ of $X$. Here and below, a strict prime of a specified model means a prime divisor on that model itself; a prime over the model may lie on any higher birational model. Thus a divisor extracted on a new model is strict on that model. The displayed intersection is therefore normal. Over the birational generic point of $Z$ its field is $k(X)(t)$.

Consider $$\widehat W=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}_X\bigoplus_{m\geq0}\mathcal O_X(-mK_X)t^m .$$ For a sufficiently divisible $r$, the analogous relative spectrum in degrees divisible by $r$ is the total space of a line bundle. It is proper over the Veronese cone: evaluation by generating sections embeds it, relative to $X$, into a product with affine space over $Z$, and $X\to Z$ is proper. The original relative spectrum is finite over this line-bundle model. Since $W$ is separated over its Veronese cone, the resulting map $\widehat W\to W$ is proper. It is birational by the fraction-field calculation. The same prime inequalities give normality of $\widehat W$.

Over the smooth locus of $X$, if $s$ is a local rational function with $\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}(s)=K_X$, the regular fibre coordinate is $st$, and the displayed form is a nowhere-vanishing top form. The complement of this locus in $\widehat W$ has codimension at least two, as is also seen by the finite map to the line-bundle model.

For completeness, the local singularities of $\widehat W$ are klt. Trivialize $rK_X$ locally and take the normalized cyclic cover adjoining $s$ with $s^r$ equal to its rational trivializing function. Because the coefficients of the Weil divisor $K_X$ are integral, this cover is quasi-etale. Finite crepant pullback preserves klt singularities. On the index cover, form the line-bundle algebra (the normalized reflexive pullback) with coordinate $y=st$. The deck group acts on $y$ with the same character as on $s$, keeping $t$ invariant; the form $\Omega=(\omega/s)\wedge dy$ is invariant. Its cyclic invariants recover the original algebra, by the codimension-one regularity inequalities. The cover of total spaces is again quasi-etale. The finite-cover discrepancy formula consequently proves that $\widehat W$ is klt. We have $\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}_{\widehat W}(\Omega)=0$, and pushing forward gives $\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}_W(\Omega)=0$. Thus the proper birational map is crepant, and $W$ is klt as well. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-a-cone-gauss"></a>
<div id="a:cone-gauss" class="lemma">

**Lemma 9** (Gaussian discrepancy). *For a prime divisor $P$ over $X$, extend $P$ by the Gauss rule with $t$-weight $q-P(K_X)$. If $q\geq0$, the extension $\widetilde P$ is centred on $W$, and $$\widetilde P(gt^m)=mq+P(\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}_X(g)-mK_X),\qquad
 A_W(\widetilde P)=q+A_X(P).$$ If $q$ is rational it is divisorial up to positive scaling. Every $D$-invariant divisorial valuation centred on $W$ has this form, allowing a positive multiple of $P$, or has trivial restriction to $k(X)$, in which case its discrepancy is $q$. The ideal $I_n=(\mathcal A_n)\subset\mathcal A$ satisfies $(W,I_n^{1/n})$ lc.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-a-cone-gauss"><summary>Proof of stmt-a-cone-gauss</summary>

*Proof.* The value formula follows from the assigned weight of $t$. The divisor $\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}_X(g)-mK_X$ is effective and rational Cartier, so the values are nonnegative. A Gauss extension is Abhyankar; wedging with $d\log t$ preserves form discrepancy, while multiplication by $t$ adds $q-P(K_X)$. Since $A_\omega(P)=A_X(P)+P(K_X)$, this proves the discrepancy formula. The rational-weight assertion follows from Lemma <a href="#a:abhyankar" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="a:abhyankar">7</a>.

An invariant valuation evaluates a sum of different torus characters by their minimum: finite torus translates separate the character components. It is therefore Gaussian. Properness gives a centre on $X$ for its restriction, and Lemma <a href="#a:abhyankar" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="a:abhyankar">7</a> applies. The same formulas hold for this restriction and for the trivial one. The corresponding $q$ is nonnegative: a sufficiently divisible relatively free anticanonical section can be chosen with zero divisor order at the centre, and its value on $W$ is its degree times $q$. The lc assertion reduces to $$\frac1n P\bigl(|-nK_X|\bigr)\leq A_X(P),$$ which is our choice of $n$. Invariant divisors suffice: take an equivariant log resolution of the invariant ideal; the connected torus fixes its finitely many prime components.

We shall also use general members of these systems. On a log resolution, the fixed part is the ideal’s order and the residual system is free. A general member with coefficient $1/n<1$ is transverse to the fixed support and preserves the lc inequalities. The same choice in a finite-dimensional generating subspace can therefore be made lc on both $X$ and $W$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-13"></a>
#### An lc place at the closed vertex

We need a bounded hypersurface statement only for a fixed-index klt germ. It is the argument used in (Chen 2025, proof of Proposition 5.1) <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[Chen](#ref-Chen)].

<a id="stmt-a-index-hypersurface"></a>
<div id="a:index-hypersurface" class="lemma">

**Lemma 10** (A hypersurface on a fixed-index germ). *Given $p\geq2$ and an integer $N>0$, there is $\tau(p,N)>0$ with the following property. If $(U,G)$ is klt near a closed point $x$, $G\geq0$, $\dim U=p$, and $N(K_U+G)$ is Cartier, then, after shrinking at $x$, an effective Cartier divisor $H_x$ through $x$ satisfies $(U,G+\tau(p,N)H_x)$ lc.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-a-index-hypersurface"><summary>Proof of stmt-a-index-hypersurface</summary>

*Proof.* Take a plt blowup extracting only $E$ over $x$, as in (Chen 2025, Lemma 2.27) <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[Chen](#ref-Chen)]. For its strict boundary $G_Y$, $-E$ and $-(K_Y+G_Y+E)$ are relatively ample rational Cartier divisors. Decreasing the coefficient of $E$ slightly gives a klt relative log Fano pair, so $Y$ is of Fano type over $U$. The coefficients of $G_Y$ belong to the finite $1/N$-grid below one. The relative monotone lc complement theorem (Birkar 2019, Theorem 1.8) <a id="pm-cite-33"></a>[[BirkarComplements](#ref-BirkarComplements)] gives a bounded-index lc complement of $G_Y+E$ near $x$. Its coefficient at $E$ remains one.

Push the complemented boundary down. The bounded-index principal identity pushes down as well and makes the pullback crepant, so the result is an lc boundary $G^+\geq G$ with $E$ an lc place. For a bounded common multiple $L$ of its complement index and $N$, the divisor $H_x=L(G^+-G)$ is effective Cartier. It passes through $x$: its order at $E$ is $L A_{(U,G)}(E)>0$. Taking $\tau=1/L$, and then a common smaller positive choice over the finitely many bounded indices, proves the claim. ◻

</details>

In the next proposition, $N$ is a cutoff tending to infinity along the contradiction sequence; it is independent of the final complement index in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

<a id="stmt-a-initial-lc-place"></a>
<div id="a:initial-lc-place" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:A2"></a>
**Proposition 11** (A centred lc place detecting the bad sequence). *After passing to a subsequence, there are divisorial valuations $u^0$ centred at $o$, and integers $N\to\infty$, such that $$u^0(I_n)=nA_W(u^0),\qquad
 u^0(\mathfrak m_z)\geq N A_W(u^0).
 \label{eq:A2}$$ Here and below $\mathfrak m_z$ denotes also its extension to $\mathcal A$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-a-initial-lc-place"><summary>Proof of stmt-a-initial-lc-place</summary>

*Proof.* Choose $N$ divisible by $n$, tending to infinity slowly enough that the bad divisors in the contradiction sequence satisfy $$\frac{P(\mathfrak m_z)}{A_X(P)}>\frac{N}{\tau(p,N)}.$$ This is a diagonal choice: each fixed $N$ is allowed before passing sufficiently far along the bad sequence. For each individual algebra, choose $M>N$ sufficiently divisible that the degree-$M$ elements cut out the irrelevant locus set-theoretically. Set $$I=(\mathcal A_N,\mathfrak m_z,\mathcal A_M).$$ Its radical is $\mathfrak m_o=\mathfrak m_z\mathcal A+\mathcal A_{>0}$. Since $I_n^{N/n}\subset I$, Lemma <a href="#a:cone-gauss" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="a:cone-gauss">9</a> implies $(W,I^{1/N})$ lc.

Suppose it were klt near $o$. A general element $g$ of a finite generating space of $I$ would give $G=N^{-1}\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}(g)$ klt there. Indeed the moving part has coefficient $1/N<1$ on a log resolution. The canonical generator of $W$ shows that $N(K_W+G)$ is Cartier. Lemma <a href="#a:index-hypersurface" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="a:index-hypersurface">10</a> supplies an effective Cartier hypersurface $H_o$ through $o$ for which $(W,G+\tau(p,N)H_o)$ is lc.

Choose rational $q$ with $$\frac{A_X(P)}{\tau(p,N)}<q\leq\frac{P(\mathfrak m_z)}N .$$ The Gauss extension $\widetilde P$ has positive value on every positive-degree element and on $\mathfrak m_z$, hence is centred at $o$. It has value at least $Nq$ on $I$, whereas its bare discrepancy is $q+A_X(P)$. Consequently $$A_{(W,G)}(\widetilde P)\leq A_X(P).$$ Moreover $\widetilde P(\mathfrak m_o)\geq q$: each positive-degree homogeneous term has value at least its degree times $q$, and each degree-zero term vanishing at $z$ has value at least $Nq$. A local equation of $H_o$ therefore has value at least $q$. This contradicts lc, since $$A_{(W,G)}(\widetilde P)\leq A_X(P)
       <\tau(p,N)q\leq\tau(p,N)\widetilde P(H_o).$$

Thus $I^{1/N}$ is lc but not klt at $o$. An lc divisor on a log resolution is centred there, because $I$ is supported at $o$ and $W$ is klt elsewhere. For this divisor, $$A_W(u^0)=\frac{u^0(I)}N
       \leq\frac{u^0(I_n)}n\leq A_W(u^0).$$ Both inequalities are equalities. Since $\mathfrak m_z\subset I$, the second inequality in <a href="#eq:A2" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:A2">[eq:A2]</a> follows too. ◻

</details>

<a id="a:minimum"></a>
### Exact normalized-volume minimization with an lc constraint

The principal point of this section is that a constrained minimum is already an ordinary klt minimum for a sufficiently small positive perturbation. Convergence to the constraint would not suffice for the finite-generation argument.

<a id="pm-h-15"></a>
#### The ordinary klt inputs

For a valuation $u$ centred at a closed point $x$ of a $p$-dimensional germ, put $$\mathfrak a_m(u)=\{F:u(F)\geq m\},\qquad
 \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(u)=\lim_{m\to\infty}
       \frac{p!\,\dim_k(\mathcal O_{U,x}/\mathfrak a_m(u))}{m^p}.$$ The limit exists for these valuation-ideal families (Cutkosky 2014, Theorem 1.1 and Corollary 5.6) <a id="pm-cite-34"></a>[[CutkoskyVolumes](#ref-CutkoskyVolumes)]; the local rings here are excellent and normal, so their completions are reduced. For a klt pair with discrepancy $A$, the normalized volume is $A(u)^p\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(u)$.

We use the following established results in their ordinary klt setting. The minimum is attained at a unique ray, its valuation is quasi-monomial, and its associated graded algebra is finitely generated and defines a klt K-semistable log Fano cone. The induced grading again minimizes: see (Blum 2018, Main Theorem and Section 7.3) <a id="pm-cite-35"></a>[[BlumExistence](#ref-BlumExistence)] <a id="pm-cite-36"></a>[[XuQuasimonomial](#ref-XuQuasimonomial)] <a id="pm-cite-37"></a>[[XuZhuangUniqueness](#ref-XuZhuangUniqueness)], (Xu 2020, Theorem 1.2 and Remark 3.8), (Xu and Zhuang 2021, Theorem 1.1), and (Xu and Zhuang 2025, Theorems 1.1–1.2), with the cone characterization in (Li and Xu 2018, Theorem 3.5) <a id="pm-cite-38"></a>[[LiXuStable](#ref-LiXuStable)]. For the bare klt germ, the estimates $$\widehat{\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}}_U(u)\geq c_U\frac{A_U(u)}{u(\mathfrak m_x)},\qquad
 u(F)\leq C_U A_U(u)\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_{\mathfrak m_x}(F)$$ are the properness and log Izumi estimates of (Li 2018, Theorems 1.1–1.2) <a id="pm-cite-39"></a>[[LiProperness](#ref-LiProperness)]. Their constants may depend on this individual germ.

If the boundary is a rational nonnegative combination of divisors of regular equations, its specialized boundary is the corresponding combination of divisors of their initial equations. The precise simultaneous specialization, including possible component multiplicities, is proved in Lemma <a href="#a:boundary-specialization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="a:boundary-specialization">17</a>. Thus all the uses below are of effective rational klt pairs.

<a id="stmt-a-minimizer-comparison"></a>
<div id="a:minimizer-comparison" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:A3"></a>
**Lemma 12** (A uniform comparison at a minimizer). *Let $b$ minimize normalized volume for a $p$-dimensional klt pair. For every centred quasi-monomial valuation $u$ and every nonzero $F\in\mathcal O_{U,x}$, $$u(F)\leq C_p\frac{A(u)}{A(b)}\,b(F),
 \label{eq:A3}$$ where $C_p$ depends only on $p$. Multiplying the discrepancy function by a common positive constant does not change this inequality.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-a-minimizer-comparison"><summary>Proof of stmt-a-minimizer-comparison</summary>

*Proof.* The relative expected-vanishing criterion (Xu and Zhuang 2021, Lemma 3.3, Definition 3.4 and Theorem 3.10) <a id="pm-cite-40"></a>[[XuZhuangUniqueness](#ref-XuZhuangUniqueness)] gives $A(u)\geq S(b;u)$. On $Q_m=\mathcal O_{U,x}/\mathfrak a_m(b)$, let $W_m(b;u)$ be the sum of the jumping weights of the induced $u$-filtration, with integer rounding if desired. Then $$\frac{S(b;u)}{A(b)}
       =\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac{W_m(b;u)}{W_m(b;b)}.$$ The induced filtration is the image filtration: its dimension at level $q$ is $$\dim_k\frac{\mathfrak a_q(u)}
               {\mathfrak a_q(u)\cap\mathfrak a_m(b)}.$$ Filtering this image by the $b$-grading gives the same dimension, so no compatibility of bases is being assumed.

Suppose $b(F)>0$, and put $k_m=\lfloor m/(2b(F))\rfloor$. Multiplication by $F^{k_m}$ embeds $\mathcal O_{U,x}/\mathfrak a_{m-k_m b(F)}(b)$ into $Q_m$. Every vector in this image has induced $u$-level at least $k_m u(F)$. The image dimension is asymptotic to $2^{-p}\dim_k Q_m$, whereas $W_m(b;b)\leq m\dim_k Q_m$. Thus $$\frac{S(b;u)}{A(b)}
       \geq 2^{-p-1}\frac{u(F)}{b(F)}.$$ The integer-rounding errors vanish in this limit. This proves <a href="#eq:A3" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:A3">[eq:A3]</a>, for example with $C_p=2^{p+1}$. A unit has value zero at both valuations. ◻

</details>

A second proof for canonical-generator germs with Cartier-equation boundaries appears in Section <a href="#a:comparison-cone-proof" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="a:comparison-cone-proof">5.4</a>, after the specialization and torus-field constructions it uses.

<a id="pm-h-16"></a>
#### The constrained problem

<a id="stmt-a-exact-penalization"></a>
<div id="a:exact-penalization" class="proposition">

**Proposition 13** (Exact penalization). *Let $x\in U$ be an affine klt germ of dimension $p$, and let $I_0,J$ be nonzero ideals. Suppose $c>0$ is rational, $(U,I_0^c)$ is lc, and an lc quasi-monomial place is centred at $x$. Let $b_0\geq1$ and $c'\geq0$ be rational, and assume $$a(u)=b_0A_U(u)-c'u(J),\qquad A_U(u)\leq a(u)\leq b_0A_U(u).$$ The minimum of $a(u)^p\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(u)$ among centred quasi-monomial lc places of $I_0^c$ is attained at a unique ray. This same ray minimizes normalized volume for a family of ordinary effective rational klt pairs for every sufficiently small positive rational parameter. In particular its associated graded is finitely generated. It is invariant, after an invariant normalization, under any torus preserving $x,I_0,J$.*

*All smallness thresholds and constants in the proof may depend on the fixed germ and ideals.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-a-exact-penalization"><summary>Proof of stmt-a-exact-penalization</summary>

*Proof.* *From ideals to ordinary klt minimizers.* Choose a common log resolution of $U,I_0,J,\mathfrak m_x$. Replace the order of each ideal by the average orders of sufficiently many general elements in a prescribed finite-dimensional generating space. Write the resulting functions as $$C=A_U-B_0,\qquad a_D=b_0A_U-B_J,$$ where $B_0$ and $B_J$ are rational nonnegative combinations of the divisors of the chosen regular equations, including the coefficients $c$ and $c'$. The resolving support and these general moving hypersurfaces form a simple normal crossing divisor $\Phi$: choose successively transverse members on its finitely many strata. Dividing each coefficient among enough members makes every new moving coefficient sufficiently small.

The old component orders are unchanged. The simple normal crossing discrepancy tests therefore give $$C\geq0,\qquad a_D\geq A_U/2,\qquad a_D\leq b_0A_U.$$ They also give $C=0$ exactly on the original ideal-lc places and $a_D=a$ there. Indeed the discrepancy expression for $C$ has nonnegative residual term and component terms, strictly positive on every added moving component, including those used for $J$. An ideal-lc place has $C=0$ by comparison with the fixed ideal orders; conversely, $C=0$ forces all these moving orders to vanish and recovers the original ideal equality. Every chosen equation then has exactly its ideal’s order.

For positive rational $t'$, sufficiently small, the function $$\frac{C+t'a_D}{1+t'b_0}$$ is the discrepancy of the effective rational klt pair with boundary $$\Delta_{t'}=\frac{B_0+t'B_J}{1+t'b_0}.$$ Let $u=u_{t'}$ minimize its normalized volume, with $u(\mathfrak m_x)=1$. Comparison with one fixed centred lc place and $a_D\geq A_U/2$ bounds $A_U(u)^p\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(u)$ independently of $t'$. Properness bounds $A_U(u)$; log Izumi consequently bounds $\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(u)$ away from zero. The same comparison now gives $C(u)=O(t')$.

*Retraction to the lc strata.* Let $\Sigma$ consist of the coefficient-one components for $C$ on the fixed resolution. Use the monomial retraction of (Jonsson and Mustaţă 2012, Lemma 4.7 and Corollary 4.8) <a id="pm-cite-41"></a>[[JM](#ref-JM)]: retract $u$ to the generic stratum of $\Sigma$ through its centre, retaining the same positive normal weights, and call the retraction $w$. The component formulas give $$\begin{aligned}
 d_{\log}:=A_{(Y,\Sigma)}(u)&\leq C_1C(u),&
 C(w)&=0,\\
 a_D(w)&\leq a_D(u),&
 w(\mathfrak m_x)&\geq1-C_1C(u).
 \end{aligned}$$ To see the first bound, express $C$ as $A_{(Y,\Phi)}$ plus the component values multiplied by their discrepancies. These coefficients are zero exactly on $\Sigma$, and positive on the omitted components. For $d_{\log}$, the omitted coefficients are instead one. The formula for $a_D$ has a nonnegative residual term and positive component coefficients. The maximal ideal is a fixed component sum. These observations give all four comparisons. In particular, for small $t'$, $w$ is nontrivial and centred at $x$.

<a id="eq:A4"></a>
Retraction has removed the lc defect and has not increased $a_D$. To compare the two objectives, we must also control its possible increase in volume. We prove the following quantitative claim for the minimizers $u=u_{t'}$ and their retractions $w$ constructed above: $$u(F)\leq(1+C_2C(u))w(F)
                  \qquad(0\ne F\in\mathcal O_{U,x}).
 \label{eq:A4}$$ The constant $C_2$ is independent of $F$ and of sufficiently small $t'$ on this fixed resolved germ. We first isolate a polynomial’s least normal terms by a differential operator of controlled order, then bound the value lost under differentiation. A local truncation will give the claim for every regular $F$.

<a id="eq:A5"></a>
*Polynomial isolation.* Cover the relevant strata by finitely many etale-coordinate charts $(x_1,\ldots,x_s,y_1,\ldots,y_{p-s})$, with $x_i=0$ the components of $\Sigma$ through the centre and $w_i=u(x_i)>0$. For a polynomial $G$ of degree at most $M$ in fixed affine generators of $k[U]$ vanishing at $x$, we claim $$u(G)-w(G)\leq C_3(1+M)d_{\log}.
 \label{eq:A5}$$ We will obtain a differential expression with $u$-value exactly $w(G)$, using $O(1+M)$ tangential and logarithmic normal derivatives. The order bound must remain independent of the smallest positive normal weight; otherwise it would not control the minimizers as their weights approach a face.

Expand $G$ normally in the $x_i$ at the generic stratum. Etaleness identifies the coefficient field as the finite separable residue-field lift over $k(y)$. Its normal Taylor coefficients are restrictions of regular lifted $x$-derivatives divided by factorials; they are regular on the stratum.
